GCTS + RL · Research notebookGUARDED HILBERT I.2 · EXTERNAL E CONTROL

SYMBOLIC INCIDENCE · EXACT THEORIES · EVERY RETURNED STEP CHECKED

Uniqueness becomes
a reusable interface.

The external prover discovers a genuine incidence consequence. It uses an admitted uniqueness interface, which also makes the complete proof check cheaper.

The target uses universally quantified line uniqueness, two distinct common points, and an additional incidence. Symbols denote uninterpreted objects and predicates; there are no coordinates or geometric drawing tests. This is a guarded first-order consequence of Hilbert’s incidence uniqueness axiom, not a proof of all Hilbert geometry.

Four positive derivations pass the separate complete checker: theory-only and full admitted library, each run twice. Four omitted-I.2 controls return CounterSatisfiable; a separate exact interpretation satisfies their full theories and libraries and falsifies the target. E’s proof objects, not theorem statuses alone, establish the positive result.

Exact symbolic inputAll four universal quantifiers and all Point/Line guards are retained. The target is not supplied with a derivation.
Two search lanesOriginal theory, or that theory with every admitted interface. E may use its unrestricted first-order calculus.
Full independent checkInputs, preprocessing, clause inferences and contradiction are checked. Library dependencies are proved separately.

The assertion is first encoded in symbols.

I.2-uniqueness

\(\forall \mathrm{point0}\;\bigl(\forall \mathrm{point1}\;\bigl(\forall \mathrm{line0}\;\bigl(\forall \mathrm{line1}\;\bigl(\bigl(\bigl(\operatorname{Point}(\mathrm{point0})\land \bigl(\operatorname{Point}(\mathrm{point1})\land \bigl(\operatorname{Line}(\mathrm{line0})\land \bigl(\operatorname{Line}(\mathrm{line1})\land \bigl(\neg\bigl(\mathrm{point0}=\mathrm{point1}\bigr)\land \bigl(\operatorname{Incident}(\mathrm{point0},\mathrm{line0})\land \bigl(\operatorname{Incident}(\mathrm{point1},\mathrm{line0})\land \bigl(\operatorname{Incident}(\mathrm{point0},\mathrm{line1})\land \operatorname{Incident}(\mathrm{point1},\mathrm{line1})\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\Rightarrow \mathrm{line0}=\mathrm{line1}\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\)

common-distinct-points

\(\bigl(\operatorname{Point}(\mathrm{A})\land \bigl(\operatorname{Point}(\mathrm{B})\land \bigl(\operatorname{Line}(\mathrm{u})\land \bigl(\operatorname{Line}(\mathrm{v})\land \bigl(\neg\bigl(\mathrm{A}=\mathrm{B}\bigr)\land \bigl(\operatorname{Incident}(\mathrm{A},\mathrm{u})\land \bigl(\operatorname{Incident}(\mathrm{B},\mathrm{u})\land \bigl(\operatorname{Incident}(\mathrm{A},\mathrm{v})\land \operatorname{Incident}(\mathrm{B},\mathrm{v})\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\)

extra-incidence

\(\operatorname{Incident}(\mathrm{C},\mathrm{u})\)

Target

\(\operatorname{Incident}(\mathrm{C},\mathrm{v})\)

The Point/Line predicates explicitly guard the two points and two lines in the uniqueness premise. Ordinary constants are not inherently distinct; the common-point hypothesis explicitly states distinctness. Equality substitution needs no separate typing hypothesis for the additional object. Existence, order, congruence, parallel and continuity axioms are absent.

The interpretation follows Hilbert’s incidence axioms in Wilkins’s parallel text: uniqueness of a line through two distinct points supplies the relevant constraint. The source notebook uses a one-sorted first-order encoding with explicit guards, rather than asserting the full sorted Hilbert axiom system.

Search cost and proof-checking cost are different.

AssertionInventoryEvidenceE binary msProof checking secondsReturned lines, both runs
incidence-transporttheory onlychecked derivation\(3.553\text{ to }4.861\)\(5.461\text{ to }5.492\)26 / 26
incidence-transportcomplete librarychecked derivation\(4.688\text{ to }4.755\)\(0.008\text{ to }0.008\)25 / 25
transport-no-uniquenesstheory onlycountermodel audited\(3.456\text{ to }3.592\)\(0.000\text{ to }0.000\)—
transport-no-uniquenesscomplete librarycountermodel audited\(3.650\text{ to }3.663\)\(0.000\text{ to }0.000\)—

The library proofs use one admitted ground uniqueness interface. Their returned traces contain one fewer line, but the larger effect is in our generic validator: checking the theory-only quantified preprocessing takes about five and a half seconds, while the library derivation takes about eight milliseconds. This is a checker implementation cost, not evidence that E’s search became comparably faster.

Fresh validation of the complete positive library has an upfront cost of about six seconds, and is charged separately. Reuse may amortize that cost over later queries. Existing proof search also benefits from reusable lemmas; this observation alone does not distinguish GCTS from it.

The GCTS geometry track’s eight positive ordering runs all take the same thirty attempted placements. Their exact finite catalog, four-row boundary, native checker and compiler costs differ from E’s unrestricted calculus and separate first-order checker. There is no formal speed ratio or GCTS+RL superiority claim. A three-row GCTS boundary is the same logical assertion and is therefore not counted as another external logical control.

The interface that E actually selected.

l5 · hilbert-c5fb56d2660bc436ad926cced602abc69a7383210af2929d915464f2e74646d9

\(\bigl(\bigl(\operatorname{Point}(\mathrm{A})\land \bigl(\operatorname{Point}(\mathrm{B})\land \bigl(\operatorname{Line}(\mathrm{u})\land \bigl(\operatorname{Line}(\mathrm{v})\land \bigl(\neg\bigl(\mathrm{A}=\mathrm{B}\bigr)\land \bigl(\operatorname{Incident}(\mathrm{A},\mathrm{u})\land \bigl(\operatorname{Incident}(\mathrm{B},\mathrm{u})\land \bigl(\operatorname{Incident}(\mathrm{A},\mathrm{v})\land \operatorname{Incident}(\mathrm{B},\mathrm{v})\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\Rightarrow \mathrm{u}=\mathrm{v}\bigr)\)

Its dependency is I.2-uniqueness. The independent check proves this interface from that exact universally quantified axiom. Native admission query 33 is disclosed reuse.

The verification scope remains explicit.

The separate validator forms each local entailment obligation, applies polarity-correct negation normal form and fresh scoped witness transformations, and produces finite universal ground instances. It accepts only a certified propositional/equality-congruence refutation of those instances. Satisfiable instances remain unknown; they do not justify a quantified validity claim or a finite-domain extrapolation. Equality-conflict proof leaves replay with a separate relation-closure algorithm.

Actual library interfaces are checked from the axioms used by their definitions. In particular, uniqueness interfaces depend on the unchanged universally quantified I.2 axiom; they are not treated as pure logical tautologies. Every reused native admission is bound to its exact theory, definition and request digest. Nonempty unexported schemas, unsupported theory fields, missing parents and unsupported introduced symbols fail closed.

All four actual E derivations have complete accepted logical checks, including their preprocessing. The E traces are not translated into native Wang tiles and do not claim native Wang acceptance. The geometry track’s native computation and point/candidate graph audit are separate evidence. At the external input freeze, the geometry snapshot reported native acceptance with full independent native replay still pending. This report does not upgrade that pending native audit into completion.

E’s official documentation notes that bundled checkproof omits non-clausal steps; it was not used to certify these runs. The input and proof syntax follows the official TPTP language. The parser and logical transformations in our independent checker remain trusted implementation code, not a formally verified kernel.

All recorded costs remain visible.

  • E binary time across all eight runs: \(0.032218\) seconds.
  • Fresh measurement worker time: \(0.287231\) seconds.
  • Returned-proof checking: \(10.968220\) seconds.
  • Fresh interface and countermodel checking: \(5.855686\) seconds.
  • Full orchestration: \(17.176400\) seconds.
  • Fresh audit and corruptions: \(30.720443\) seconds.
  • Peak E resident memory: \(4.812500\) MiB.

E binary SHA-256: a2fc18a82ed1dbdcdd37eefeb5c71ccad1e8fc48662466f61372fc0be5947f21; official source commit 3b7afc70fe77d3118bb95144c4735ca45f5f31cf; recorded version E 3.5.1-DEBUG Countess Grey (25808ee34733f8641fc5962acc203d453d9dbf1a). This is the earlier DEBUG build; resident bytes use independent platform-aware per-child accounting.

The source snapshots, exact TPTP inputs, every admitted interface, method order and budgets were frozen before recipient execution. Each E process has a sixty-second CPU limit, sixty-five-second external wall cutoff and requested one-GiB memory limit. Repetition two reverses lane order. Processes are fresh, filesystem caches warm, and concurrent research workloads prevent tightly controlled latency attribution.

The pinned E build and its earlier measured rebuild are reused. They are not charged as a fresh rebuild here. Geometry compilation/native admission is also disclosed reuse and was not reexecuted by this external experiment. New independent logical interface checks, proof checks and corruption replay are charged. The whole orchestration includes snapshot parsing, input export, provenance checks, worker startup and serialization.

Reproducible evidence, with no skipped proof lines.

The fresh audit checks four successful derivations and their one hundred and two returned lines, independently checks forty supplied interfaces, and reevaluates both control libraries’ countermodels. It rejects twelve corrupted traces, eighty-two corrupted local refutation trees, and both unexported-schema and unexported-theory-field fixtures.

Reproduce from the repository root
# Use the official E source/build pinned in the earlier external-control report.
python3 - <<'PY'
import gzip,json,pathlib
bundle=json.loads(gzip.decompress(pathlib.Path('docs/research/gcts-rl-renewal/external_hilbert_control_data.json.gz').read_bytes()))
destination=pathlib.Path('.gcts-active/external-hilbert-reproduce-inputs')
destination.mkdir(parents=True,exist_ok=True)
for name,text in bundle['source_files'].items():
    (destination/name).write_bytes(text.encode('utf8'))
PY
PYTHONPYCACHEPREFIX=/private/tmp/gcts-native-wang-pycache python3 research/gcts-rl-renewal/external_hilbert_run.py --source .gcts-active/external-hilbert-reproduce-inputs --output .gcts-active/external-hilbert-reproduce --e /private/tmp/gcts-external-eprover-20261011/PROVER/eprover
PYTHONPYCACHEPREFIX=/private/tmp/gcts-native-wang-pycache python3 research/gcts-rl-renewal/external_hilbert_audit.py --output .gcts-active/external-hilbert-reproduce

The evidence bundle includes the original snapshot objects and exact UTF-8 source text, native request/boundary evidence and actual TSTP outputs. The extraction above preserves the pinned source digests; reserializing the parsed objects is a different input artifact. No claim of an unbounded universal mathematical proof compiler, full Hilbert geometry, plane tiling or substitution-rule discovery follows from this experiment.
